Morphology & anvaya

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The gods’ hymn on the Himālaya (5.9–82) is over, and Medhas, still speaking to King Suratha, returns to the story: what happened while the gods were praising.

Verse 5.84

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एवं स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानां तत्र पार्वती।स्नातुमभ्याययौ तोये जाह्नव्या नृपनन्दन॥⁠८४⁠॥

evaṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ devānāṃ tatra pārvatī |snātumabhyāyayau toye jāhnavyā nṛpanandana ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एवम् · स्तवादियुक्तानाम् · देवानाम् · तत्र · पार्वतीस्नातुम् · अभ्याययौ · तोये · जाह्नव्याः · नृपनन्दन

evam · stavādiyuktānām · devānām · tatra · pārvatīsnātum · abhyāyayau · toye · jāhnavyāḥ · nṛpanandana

Word by word

  1. एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  2. स्तवादियुक्तानाम्stavādiyuktānāmengaged in praise and the restm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवानाम्
  3. देवानाम्devānām(while) the gods (were)m. gen. pl. of देव; with स्तवादियुक्तानाम् a genitive absolute
  4. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place, from तद्)
  5. पार्वतीpārvatīPārvatī, the daughter of the mountainf. nom. sg. of पार्वती (taddhita from पर्वत ‘mountain’); subject of अभ्याययौ
  6. स्नातुम्snātumto batheinfinitive (tumun) of √स्ना (adādi, 2P ‘to bathe’); purpose
  7. अभ्याययौabhyāyayaucame (towards that place)अभि + आ + √या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. तोयेtoyein the watern. loc. sg. of तोय
  9. जाह्नव्याःjāhnavyāḥof the Jāhnavī (the Gaṅgā)f. gen. sg. of जाह्नवी (taddhita: ‘daughter of Jahnu’); qualifies तोये
  10. नृपनन्दनnṛpanandanaO prince (Suratha)m. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

While the gods were thus engaged in praise and the rest, there Pārvatī came, O son of a king, to bathe in the water of the Jāhnavī.

In readable EnglishWhile the gods were thus praising, O prince, Pārvatī came there to bathe in the waters of the Gaṅgā.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं स्तवादियुक्तानां evaṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ → एवम् + स्तवादियुक्तानाम्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so also स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानां and देवानां तत्र.

  • स्तवादि stavādi → स्तव + आदि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • जाह्नव्या नृपनन्दन jāhnavyā nṛpanandana → जाह्नव्याः + नृपनन्दन

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced sound: the visarga (as रु) becomes y, and a y so produced is always dropped before a consonant, leaving जाह्नव्या.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • स्तवादियुक्तानाम् stavādiyuktānām

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (its first member स्तवादि is a bahuvrīhi: स्तवः आदिः येषां ते)

    Members
    स्तवादि + युक्त
    Vigraha
    स्तवादिभिः युक्ताः, तेषाम् stavādibhiḥ yuktāḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “joined with praise and the like”
    In the verse
    occupied with hymns and other acts of worship

  • नृपनन्दन nṛpanandana

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    नृप + नन्दन
    Vigraha
    नृपस्य नन्दनः nṛpasya nandanaḥ
    Literally
    “a king’s delight, i.e. a king’s son”
    In the verse
    O prince — Medhas’s address to Suratha
Morphology & anvaya

  • अभ्याययौ abhyāyayau

    Perfect of √या (ययौ, with the -au ending of ā-roots) behind two prefixes: अभि + आ, where i becomes y before ā (अभ्या-). ‘Came up to, approached’.

  • स्नातुम् snātum

    Infinitive in -tum of √स्ना, expressing purpose with a verb of motion: ‘came to bathe’.

  • देवानाम् devānām

    With the participle-like adjective स्तवादियुक्तानाम् it forms a genitive absolute: ‘the gods being so engaged’, i.e. ‘while the gods were praising’.

Anvaya · prose order

नृपनन्दन, एवं देवानां स्तवादियुक्तानां (सताम्) तत्र पार्वती जाह्नव्याः तोये स्नातुम् अभ्याययौ।

nṛpanandana, evaṃ devānāṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ (satām) tatra pārvatī jāhnavyāḥ toye snātum abhyāyayau |

A genitive absolute (एवं … देवानाम्) sets the time; the main clause is पार्वती … अभ्याययौ with the purpose infinitive स्नातुम्; नृपनन्दन is the narrator’s address.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The scene shifts while the hymn is still in the air: Pārvatī, Śiva’s consort and the daughter of Himavat, comes down to the Gaṅgā to bathe at the very place where the gods are praying. The genitive absolute ties the two events together.

Traditional interpretation

Chapter 4 announced this episode in advance: the Goddess would arise again ‘from the body of Gaurī’ to kill Śumbha and Niśumbha (4.41, गौरीदेहात्). Pārvatī’s coming to the river is the occasion for that promise to be kept.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the same name, पार्वती (name 246). The two works share the Śākta identification of the Goddess with Himavat’s daughter; they remain distinct texts.

Verse 5.85

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

साब्रवीत्तान् सुरान् सुभ्रूर्भवद्भिः स्तूयतेऽत्र का।शरीरकोशतश्चास्याः समुद्भूताब्रवीच्छिवा॥⁠८५⁠॥

sābravīttān surān subhrūrbhavadbhiḥ stūyate'tra kā |śarīrakośataścāsyāḥ samudbhūtābravīcchivā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

सा · अब्रवीत् · तान् · सुरान् · सुभ्रूः · भवद्भिः · स्तूयते · अत्र · काशरीरकोशतः · च · अस्याः · समुद्भूता · अब्रवीत् · शिवा

sā · abravīt · tān · surān · subhrūḥ · bhavadbhiḥ · stūyate · atra · kāśarīrakośataḥ · ca · asyāḥ · samudbhūtā · abravīt · śivā

Word by word

  1. साsāshe (Pārvatī)pronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of the first अब्रवीत्
  2. अब्रवीत्abravītsaid√ब्रू (adādi, 2U ‘to say’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. तान्tānthosepronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
  4. सुरान्surānto the godsm. acc. pl. of सुर; the person addressed (verbs of speaking take the addressee in the accusative)
  5. सुभ्रूःsubhrūḥthe fair-browed onef. nom. sg. of सुभ्रू; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to सा
  6. भवद्भिःbhavadbhiḥby you (sirs)honorific pronoun भवत्, m. instr. pl.; agent of the passive स्तूयते
  7. स्तूयतेstūyateis being praised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
  8. अत्रatrahereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  9. काkāwho (which woman)interrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; subject of स्तूयते
  10. शरीरकोशतःśarīrakośataḥfrom the sheath of the bodyindeclinable ablative in -tas (tasil) of the compound शरीरकोश (see Samāsa)
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  12. अस्याःasyāḥof her (Pārvatī)pronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.; depends on शरीरकोशतः
  13. समुद्भूताsamudbhūtāhaving come forthpast participle (kta) of सम् + उद् + √भू, f. nom. sg.; qualifies शिवा
  14. अब्रवीत्abravītsaid√ब्रू (adādi, 2U), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  15. शिवाśivāŚivā, the Auspicious Onef. nom. sg. of शिवा; subject of the second अब्रवीत्

Translation

She, the fair-browed one, said to those gods: ‘Who is being praised by you here?’ And Śivā, having come forth from the sheath of her body, said:

In readable EnglishThe lovely-browed Goddess asked the gods, ‘Whom are you praising here?’ And from the sheath of her own body Śivā came forth and answered:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • साब्रवीत् sābravīt → सा + अब्रवीत्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • सुभ्रूर्भवद्भिः subhrūrbhavadbhiḥ → सुभ्रूः + भवद्भिः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After a vowel other than a/ā the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a voiced consonant.

  • स्तूयतेऽत्र stūyate'tra → स्तूयते + अत्र

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is elided (avagraha).

  • शरीरकोशतश्चास्याः śarīrakośataścāsyāḥ → शरीरकोशतः + च + अस्याः

    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś (-तश्च); then च + अस्याः → चास्याः.

  • समुद्भूताब्रवीच्छिवा samudbhūtābravīcchivā → समुद्भूता + अब्रवीत् + शिवा

    vowel sandhi, then consonant sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)

    ā + a → ā; then final t before ś becomes c, and the ś after it becomes ch: अब्रवीत् शिवा → अब्रवीच्छिवा.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सुभ्रूः subhrūḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    सु + भ्रू
    Vigraha
    शोभने भ्रुवौ यस्याः सा śobhane bhruvau yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose brows are fair”
    In the verse
    the lovely-browed Goddess, Pārvatī

  • शरीरकोश śarīrakośa

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शरीर + कोश
    Vigraha
    शरीरस्य कोशः śarīrasya kośaḥ
    Literally
    “the sheath of the body”
    In the verse
    the bodily envelope, out of which the new form steps
    Alternative
    Also read as a karmadhāraya of identification, शरीरम् एव कोशः ‘the body, which is a sheath’; the sense is the same.
Morphology & anvaya

  • अब्रवीत् abravīt

    Imperfect of √ब्रू with augment a- and the connecting ī that ब्रू takes before consonant endings of the strong forms: अ-ब्रव्-ई-त्. The verse uses it twice, once for each speaker.

  • स्तूयते stūyate

    Passive present of √स्तु: the root vowel is lengthened before the passive suffix -ya- (स्तू-य-ते). With the instrumental भवद्भिः: ‘who is praised by you?’.

  • शरीरकोशतः śarīrakośataḥ

    The suffix -tas (tasil) makes an indeclinable with ablative sense: ‘from the body’s sheath’. It still governs a genitive (अस्याः), as a noun would.

Anvaya · prose order

सुभ्रूः सा तान् सुरान् अब्रवीत् — अत्र भवद्भिः का स्तूयते (इति)। अस्याः शरीरकोशतः समुद्भूता शिवा च अब्रवीत्।

subhrūḥ sā tān surān abravīt — atra bhavadbhiḥ kā stūyate (iti) | asyāḥ śarīrakośataḥ samudbhūtā śivā ca abravīt |

Two sentences of the same shape: subject, अब्रवीत्. The first carries Pārvatī’s question as direct speech; the second introduces the answer, which is given in 5.86.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Pārvatī asks whom the gods are praising, and the answer comes not from them but from a goddess who steps out of Pārvatī’s own body. The one who asks and the one who answers are of one substance; the reply follows in 5.86.

Traditional interpretation

Traditionally the question is read as the Goddess’s play (līlā): she asks as though she did not know, so that she herself may reveal that the gods’ hymn was addressed to her.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names the Goddess सुभ्रूः (name 461) and शिवा (name 53). The shared epithets belong to a common Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate.

Verse 5.86

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

स्तोत्रं ममैतत् क्रियते शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः।देवैः समेतैः समरे निशुम्भेन पराजितैः॥⁠८६⁠॥

stotraṃ mamaitat kriyate śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ |devaiḥ sametaiḥ samare niśumbhena parājitaiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

स्तोत्रम् · मम · एतत् · क्रियते · शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैःदेवैः · समेतैः · समरे · निशुम्भेन · पराजितैः

stotram · mama · etat · kriyate · śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥdevaiḥ · sametaiḥ · samare · niśumbhena · parājitaiḥ

Word by word

  1. स्तोत्रम्stotramhymn of praisen. nom. sg. of स्तोत्र (√स्तु + ṣṭran); subject of the passive क्रियते
  2. ममmamaof me, to mefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; the one praised
  3. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies स्तोत्रम्
  4. क्रियतेkriyateis being made, is offered√कृ (tanādi, 8U ‘to do, make’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
  5. शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैःśumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥdriven out by the demon Śumbham. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies देवैः
  6. देवैःdevaiḥby the godsm. instr. pl. of देव; agent of क्रियते
  7. समेतैःsametaiḥassembled togetherpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √इ ‘to come together’, m. instr. pl.; qualifies देवैः
  8. समरेsamarein battlem. loc. sg. of समर
  9. निशुम्भेनniśumbhenaby Niśumbham. instr. sg. of निशुम्भ; agent of पराजितैः
  10. पराजितैःparājitaiḥdefeatedpast participle (kta) of परा + √जि (bhvādi, 1P ‘to conquer’), m. instr. pl.; qualifies देवैः

Translation

This hymn to me is being made by the gods, assembled, driven out by the daitya Śumbha and defeated in battle by Niśumbha.

In readable English‘This hymn is offered to me by the gods, who have gathered here after the demon Śumbha drove them out and Niśumbha defeated them in battle.’

‘After’ makes explicit the order of events implied by the three participles.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स्तोत्रं ममैतत् stotraṃ mamaitat → स्तोत्रम् + मम + एतत्

    anusvāra sandhi, then vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; a + e → ai (मम + एतत् → ममैतत्).

  • दैत्यनिराकृतैः daityanirākṛtaiḥ → दैत्य + निर् + आ + कृतैः

    consonant change inside a compound (the prefix निस्) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The prefix निस् appears as निर् before the vowel of आ (its s treated as रु and sounded r): निराकृत ‘driven away, rejected’.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (its first member शुम्भदैत्य is a karmadhāraya: शुम्भः च असौ दैत्यः च)

    Members
    शुम्भदैत्य + निराकृत
    Vigraha
    शुम्भदैत्येन निराकृताः, तैः śumbhadaityena nirākṛtāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “rejected by Śumbha the daitya”
    In the verse
    the gods whom the demon Śumbha has thrown out of their realms (5.2–5)
Morphology & anvaya

  • क्रियते kriyate

    Passive present of √कृ: before the passive -ya- the root’s ṛ becomes ri (क्रि-य-ते). ‘Is being made’.

  • समेतैः sametaiḥ

    सम् + आ + √इ + kta: आ + इत → एत by guṇa (6.1.87), so समेत ‘come together, assembled’.

  • पराजितैः parājitaiḥ

    Past participle of परा + √जि, ‘conquered, defeated’, construed with the agent instrumental निशुम्भेन.

Anvaya · prose order

शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः समरे निशुम्भेन पराजितैः समेतैः देवैः एतत् मम स्तोत्रं क्रियते।

śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ samare niśumbhena parājitaiḥ sametaiḥ devaiḥ etat mama stotraṃ kriyate |

A passive sentence: subject स्तोत्रम्, verb क्रियते, agent देवैः with three qualifiers (निराकृतैः, समेतैः, पराजितैः).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śivā answers Pārvatī’s question: the hymn is hers. The gods are named by their plight — expelled by Śumbha, beaten in battle by Niśumbha — which recalls the opening of the chapter (5.2–5).

Traditional interpretation

The tradition reads the episode as a statement of identity: the Goddess the gods invoked as dwelling in all beings (5.14–80) answers from within Pārvatī herself. Asking and answering, she shows that the hymn and its object are hers.

Verse 5.87

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

शरीरकोशाद्यत्तस्याः पार्वत्या निःसृताम्बिका।कौशिकीति समस्तेषु ततो लोकेषु गीयते॥⁠८७⁠॥

śarīrakośādyattasyāḥ pārvatyā niḥsṛtāmbikā |kauśikīti samasteṣu tato lokeṣu gīyate ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

शरीरकोशात् · यत् · तस्याः · पार्वत्याः · निःसृता · अम्बिकाकौशिकी · इति · समस्तेषु · ततः · लोकेषु · गीयते

śarīrakośāt · yat · tasyāḥ · pārvatyāḥ · niḥsṛtā · ambikākauśikī · iti · samasteṣu · tataḥ · lokeṣu · gīyate

Word by word

  1. शरीरकोशात्śarīrakośātfrom the sheath of the bodym. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.85)
  2. यत्yatsince, becauseindeclinable (conjunction; correlative of ततः)
  3. तस्याःtasyāḥof thatpronoun तद्, f. gen. sg.; qualifies पार्वत्याः
  4. पार्वत्याःpārvatyāḥof Pārvatīf. gen. sg. of पार्वती; depends on शरीरकोशात्
  5. निःसृताniḥsṛtācame forthpast participle (kta) of निस् + √सृ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to flow, move’), f. nom. sg.; used as the verb of the clause
  6. अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject
  7. कौशिकीkauśikīKauśikī, ‘she of the sheath’f. nom. sg. of कौशिकी (taddhita from कोश); the name quoted by इति
  8. इतिitias, by the nameindeclinable (marks the preceding word as a name)
  9. समस्तेषुsamasteṣuin allm. loc. pl. of समस्त; qualifies लोकेषु
  10. ततःtataḥthereforeindeclinable (adverb, correlative of यत्)
  11. लोकेषुlokeṣuin the worldsm. loc. pl. of लोक
  12. गीयतेgīyateis sung of, is celebrated√गै (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sing’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

Translation

Since Ambikā came forth from the sheath of the body of that Pārvatī, she is therefore sung of in all the worlds as ‘Kauśikī’.

In readable EnglishBecause Ambikā emerged from the sheath of Pārvatī’s body, she is celebrated in all the worlds as Kauśikī.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • शरीरकोशाद्यत्तस्याः śarīrakośādyattasyāḥ → शरीरकोशात् + यत् + तस्याः

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced y; the t of यत् stays t before the t of तस्याः.

  • पार्वत्या निःसृताम्बिका pārvatyā niḥsṛtāmbikā → पार्वत्याः + निःसृता + अम्बिका

    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga (रु → y, dropped before the consonant); then ā + a → ā (निःसृताम्बिका).

  • कौशिकीति kauśikīti → कौशिकी + इति

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ī + i → ī.

  • ततो लोकेषु tato lokeṣu → ततः + लोकेषु

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शरीरकोशात् śarīrakośāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    शरीर + कोश
    Vigraha
    शरीरस्य कोशः, तस्मात् śarīrasya kośaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the sheath of the body”
    In the verse
    as in 5.85, here in the ablative case form
Morphology & anvaya

  • कौशिकी kauśikī

    Taddhita: कोश + the affix -ika with vṛddhi of the first vowel (को → कौ), feminine in -ī: ‘she who belongs to (came from) the sheath’. The verse itself gives the etymology.

  • गीयते gīyate

    Passive present of √गै: the root appears as गी before the passive -ya- (गी-य-ते), ‘is sung, is celebrated’.

  • निःसृता niḥsṛtā

    निस् + √सृ + kta, feminine: ‘issued forth’. The participle carries the action without an auxiliary verb.

Anvaya · prose order

यत् तस्याः पार्वत्याः शरीरकोशात् अम्बिका निःसृता, ततः (सा) समस्तेषु लोकेषु कौशिकी इति गीयते।

yat tasyāḥ pārvatyāḥ śarīrakośāt ambikā niḥsṛtā, tataḥ (sā) samasteṣu lokeṣu kauśikī iti gīyate |

A causal pair: यत् … निःसृता (‘since she came forth …’) and ततः … गीयते (‘therefore she is sung of …’).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas explains the name: the goddess who emerged (Śivā of 5.85, now called Ambikā) is Kauśikī because she came out of Pārvatī’s bodily sheath (kośa). From here the text mostly calls her Ambikā or Devī.

Traditional interpretation

In the Gita Press pāṭha, the dhyāna printed before chapter 5 describes the presiding Goddess of the third carita, Mahāsarasvatī, as गौरीदेहसमुद्भवाम् ‘born from the body of Gaurī’ — this very emergence. The assignment of Mahāsarasvatī to the carita is liturgical tradition.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also calls the Goddess अम्बिका (name 295). The Devī Māhātmya’s own story of Kauśikī belongs to this text; the shared name does not import the other hymn’s theology.

Verse 5.88

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तस्यां विनिर्गतायां तु कृष्णाभूत्सापि पार्वती।कालिकेति समाख्याता हिमाचलकृताश्रया॥⁠८८⁠॥

tasyāṃ vinirgatāyāṃ tu kṛṣṇābhūtsāpi pārvatī |kāliketi samākhyātā himācalakṛtāśrayā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तस्याम् · विनिर्गतायाम् · तु · कृष्णा · अभूत् · सा · अपि · पार्वतीकालिका · इति · समाख्याता · हिमाचलकृताश्रया

tasyām · vinirgatāyām · tu · kṛṣṇā · abhūt · sā · api · pārvatīkālikā · iti · samākhyātā · himācalakṛtāśrayā

Word by word

  1. तस्याम्tasyāmshe (Ambikā)pronoun तद्, f. loc. sg.; with विनिर्गतायाम् a locative absolute
  2. विनिर्गतायाम्vinirgatāyāmhaving gone outpast participle (kta) of वि + निर् + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to go’), f. loc. sg.; locative absolute
  3. तुtuand, thenindeclinable (particle marking a new step in the narrative)
  4. कृष्णाkṛṣṇādark, blackf. nom. sg. of the adjective कृष्ण; predicate of अभूत्
  5. अभूत्abhūtbecame√भू (bhvādi, 1P), luṅ (aorist), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies पार्वती
  7. अपिapialso, in turnindeclinable (particle)
  8. पार्वतीpārvatīPārvatīf. nom. sg. of पार्वती; subject
  9. कालिकाkālikāKālikā, ‘the dark one’f. nom. sg. of कालिका; the name quoted by इति
  10. इतिitias, by the nameindeclinable (marks the preceding word as a name)
  11. समाख्याताsamākhyātācalled, knownpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √ख्या (adādi, 2P ‘to tell’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies पार्वती
  12. हिमाचलकृताश्रयाhimācalakṛtāśrayāwho made her dwelling on the Himālayaf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies पार्वती

Translation

And when she had gone out, that Pārvatī too became dark; called ‘Kālikā’, she made her dwelling on the Himālaya.

In readable EnglishWhen Ambikā had come forth, Pārvatī herself turned dark. Known as Kālikā, she took up her abode on the Himālaya.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तस्यां विनिर्गतायां तु tasyāṃ vinirgatāyāṃ tu → तस्याम् + विनिर्गतायाम् + तु

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.

  • कृष्णाभूत्सापि kṛṣṇābhūtsāpi → कृष्णा + अभूत् + सा + अपि

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā twice (कृष्णाभूत्, सापि); the t of अभूत् stays unvoiced before s and is only written joined.

  • कालिकेति kāliketi → कालिका + इति

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + i → e.

  • हिमाचल himācala → हिम + अचल

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā; so also कृत + आश्रया → कृताश्रया.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हिमाचलकृताश्रया himācalakṛtāśrayā

    bahuvrīhi (feminine, -ā)

    Members
    हिमाचल + कृत + आश्रय
    Vigraha
    हिमाचले कृतः आश्रयः यया सा himācale kṛtaḥ āśrayaḥ yayā sā
    Literally
    “she by whom a dwelling has been made on the snow-mountain”
    In the verse
    abiding on the Himālaya

  • हिमाचल himācala

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अचल ‘the immovable’, i.e. a mountain, is itself a nañ tatpuruṣa: न चलः)

    Members
    हिम + अचल
    Vigraha
    हिमस्य अचलः himasya acalaḥ
    Literally
    “the mountain of snow”
    In the verse
    the Himālaya
Morphology & anvaya

  • विनिर्गतायाम् vinirgatāyām

    Locative absolute (with तस्याम्): ‘she having gone out’, i.e. ‘when she had emerged’. The participle of वि + निर् + √गम् loses the root’s m before -ta (गत).

  • अभूत् abhūt

    Root aorist of √भू with a predicate adjective: कृष्णा अभूत् ‘became dark’.

Anvaya · prose order

तस्यां तु विनिर्गतायां सा पार्वती अपि कृष्णा अभूत्, (सा) कालिका इति समाख्याता हिमाचलकृताश्रया (च अभूत्)।

tasyāṃ tu vinirgatāyāṃ sā pārvatī api kṛṣṇā abhūt, (sā) kālikā iti samākhyātā himācalakṛtāśrayā (ca abhūt) |

A locative absolute (तस्यां विनिर्गतायाम्) gives the moment; the main clause is सा पार्वती … कृष्णा अभूत्, with two further qualifiers of Pārvatī in the second line.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Once the radiant Ambikā has left her, Pārvatī becomes dark and is called Kālikā; she remains on the Himālaya. The narrative now follows Ambikā; Kālikā does not act again in this chapter.

Traditional interpretation

Kālikā here is Pārvatī after the emergence, not the Kālī who later springs from Ambikā’s brow to fight Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (7.5–6). Other Purāṇic tellings reverse the colours — the dark sheath becomes Kauśikī and Pārvatī becomes fair Gaurī — but this text says plainly that Ambikā came out and Pārvatī became dark.

Verse 5.89

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततोऽम्बिकां परं रूपं बिभ्राणां सुमनोहरम्।ददर्श चण्डो मुण्डश्च भृत्यौ शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः॥⁠८९⁠॥

tato'mbikāṃ paraṃ rūpaṃ bibhrāṇāṃ sumanoharam |dadarśa caṇḍo muṇḍaśca bhṛtyau śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · अम्बिकाम् · परम् · रूपम् · बिभ्राणाम् · सुमनोहरम्ददर्श · चण्डः · मुण्डः · च · भृत्यौ · शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः

tataḥ · ambikām · param · rūpam · bibhrāṇām · sumanoharamdadarśa · caṇḍaḥ · muṇḍaḥ · ca · bhṛtyau · śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  2. अम्बिकाम्ambikāmAmbikāf. acc. sg. of अम्बिका; object of ददर्श
  3. परम्paramsupreme, surpassingn. acc. sg. of पर; qualifies रूपम्
  4. रूपम्rūpamform, beautyn. acc. sg. of रूप; object of बिभ्राणाम्
  5. बिभ्राणाम्bibhrāṇāmbearing, possessingpresent participle (śānac) of √भृ (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to bear’), f. acc. sg.; qualifies अम्बिकाम्
  6. सुमनोहरम्sumanoharammost captivatingn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपम्
  7. ददर्शdadarśasaw√दृश् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to see’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. चण्डःcaṇḍaḥCaṇḍam. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
  9. मुण्डःmuṇḍaḥMuṇḍam. nom. sg.; proper name; subject
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. भृत्यौbhṛtyauthe two servantsm. nom. du. of भृत्य (gerundive of √भृ: ‘one to be maintained’); in apposition to चण्डः मुण्डः
  12. शुम्भनिशुम्भयोःśumbhaniśumbhayoḥof Śumbha and Niśumbham. gen. du.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on भृत्यौ

Translation

Then Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, the two servants of Śumbha and Niśumbha, saw Ambikā bearing a supreme, most captivating form.

In readable EnglishThen Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, servants of Śumbha and Niśumbha, saw Ambikā in her surpassing, enchanting beauty.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततोऽम्बिकां tato'mbikāṃ → ततः + अम्बिकाम्

    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a: the visarga (as रु) becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha): ततोऽम्बिकाम्.

  • परं रूपं paraṃ rūpaṃ → परम् + रूपम्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so also रूपं बिभ्राणां सुमनोहरम्.

  • चण्डो मुण्डश्च caṇḍo muṇḍaśca → चण्डः + मुण्डः + च

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o; -aḥ before c becomes -aś.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सुमनोहरम् sumanoharam

    prādi tatpuruṣa (an indeclinable prefix as first member)

    Members
    सु + मनोहर
    Vigraha
    सुष्ठु मनोहरम् suṣṭhu manoharam
    Literally
    “well heart-stealing”
    In the verse
    exceedingly lovely

  • मनोहर manohara

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मनस् + हर
    Vigraha
    मनः हरति इति manaḥ harati iti
    Literally
    “that which steals the mind”
    In the verse
    captivating; मनस् + हर → मनोहर by the visarga rule हशि च (6.1.114)

  • शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    शुम्भ + निशुम्भ
    Vigraha
    शुम्भः च निशुम्भः च, तयोः śumbhaḥ ca niśumbhaḥ ca, tayoḥ
    Literally
    “of Śumbha and Niśumbha”
    In the verse
    the two demon brothers
Morphology & anvaya

  • बिभ्राणाम् bibhrāṇām

    Present participle (ātmanepada, -āna) of the reduplicating root √भृ: बि-भ्र-आण, with ṇ for n after r. ‘Bearing’ a form, i.e. having it.

  • ददर्श dadarśa

    Perfect of √दृश् (द-दर्श, with guṇa). The verb is singular though it has two subjects joined by च; a verb that stands first often agrees with the nearer subject in Purāṇic narrative.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः भृत्यौ चण्डः मुण्डः च परं सुमनोहरं रूपं बिभ्राणाम् अम्बिकां ददर्श।

tataḥ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ bhṛtyau caṇḍaḥ muṇḍaḥ ca paraṃ sumanoharaṃ rūpaṃ bibhrāṇām ambikāṃ dadarśa |

Verb ददर्श, its object अम्बिकाम् with the participle phrase परं रूपं बिभ्राणाम् सुमनोहरम्, and the double subject चण्डः मुण्डः च with the apposition भृत्यौ.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa enter the story as servants of the demon brothers. Their seeing of the Goddess sets the whole uttama carita in motion: report, embassy and war.

Traditional interpretation

The same two servants lead the first army against her (7.2) and die by Kālī; Caṇḍikā then names Kālī ‘Cāmuṇḍā’ after them (7.27). The pair who first look on her beauty become the occasion of one of her best-known names.

Verse 5.90

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ताभ्यां शुम्भाय चाख्याता अतीव सुमनोहरा।काप्यास्ते स्त्री महाराज भासयन्ती हिमाचलम्॥⁠९०⁠॥

tābhyāṃ śumbhāya cākhyātā atīva sumanoharā |kāpyāste strī mahārāja bhāsayantī himācalam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ताभ्याम् · शुम्भाय · च · आख्याता · अतीव · सुमनोहराका · अपि · आस्ते · स्त्री · महाराज · भासयन्ती · हिमाचलम्

tābhyām · śumbhāya · ca · ākhyātā · atīva · sumanoharākā · api · āste · strī · mahārāja · bhāsayantī · himācalam

Word by word

  1. ताभ्याम्tābhyāmby those two (Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa)pronoun तद्, m. instr. du.; agent of आख्याता
  2. शुम्भायśumbhāyato Śumbham. dat. sg. of शुम्भ; recipient of the report
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction, linking to 5.89)
  4. आख्याताākhyātā(she) was reportedpast participle (kta) of आ + √ख्या (adādi, 2P ‘to tell’), f. nom. sg.; the understood subject is Ambikā
  5. अतीवatīvaexceedinglyindeclinable (adverb: अति + इव)
  6. सुमनोहराsumanoharālovely, captivatingf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.89); qualifies स्त्री
  7. काkāsome, a certainpronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.; with अपि indefinite
  8. अपिapi(makes का indefinite: ‘some … or other’)indeclinable (particle)
  9. आस्तेāsteis staying, sits√आस् (adādi, 2Ā ‘to sit, stay’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  10. स्त्रीstrīwomanf. nom. sg. of स्त्री; subject of आस्ते
  11. महाराजmahārājaO great kingm. voc. sg. of महाराज (compound: see Samāsa)
  12. भासयन्तीbhāsayantīilluminatingpresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √भास् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to shine’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies स्त्री
  13. हिमाचलम्himācalamthe Himālayam. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.88); object of भासयन्ती

Translation

And she was reported by those two to Śumbha: ‘Some exceedingly captivating woman, O great king, is staying (there), illuminating the Himālaya.’

In readable EnglishThey told Śumbha of her: ‘Great king, there is a woman of surpassing beauty dwelling on the Himālaya, lighting up the mountain.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ताभ्यां शुम्भाय tābhyāṃ śumbhāya → ताभ्याम् + शुम्भाय

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • चाख्याता cākhyātā → च + आख्याता

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • काप्यास्ते kāpyāste → का + अपि + आस्ते

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha, then yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    ā + a → ā (काप्-); then i before ā becomes y (अपि आस्ते → अप्यास्ते).

  • अतीव atīva → अति + इव

    vowel sandhi inside a fixed indeclinable (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i + i → ī: ‘over-like’, used as ‘exceedingly’.

Left unjoined

  • आख्याता | अतीव — Hiatus at the pāda boundary (ताभ्यां शुम्भाय चाख्याता is a complete eight-syllable pāda), which is also where the reported speech begins. Purāṇic verse often leaves vowels unjoined across the pāda break; the printed text keeps them apart.
Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महाराज mahārāja

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + राजन्
    Vigraha
    महान् राजा mahān rājā
    Literally
    “great king”
    In the verse
    Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa’s address to Śumbha; in the compound महत् becomes महा- and राजन् becomes राज (an a-stem)
Morphology & anvaya

  • आख्याता ākhyātā

    Feminine past participle used as a finite verb: ‘(she) was reported’. The subject, Ambikā, is understood from 5.89; what they said follows as direct speech.

  • आस्ते āste

    Present of the ātmanepada root √आस्: ‘sits, stays, is there’.

  • भासयन्ती bhāsayantī

    Causative stem भासय- (‘make shine’) + śatṛ, feminine -ī: ‘making the Himālaya shine’.

Anvaya · prose order

ताभ्यां च शुम्भाय (सा) आख्याता — महाराज, अतीव सुमनोहरा का अपि स्त्री हिमाचलं भासयन्ती आस्ते।

tābhyāṃ ca śumbhāya (sā) ākhyātā — mahārāja, atīva sumanoharā kā api strī himācalaṃ bhāsayantī āste |

A passive report clause (ताभ्यां … आख्याता), then the beginning of the servants’ speech: a woman (स्त्री, with three qualifiers) is (आस्ते).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The servants’ report to Śumbha begins; it runs to 5.100. They know nothing of who she is — she is ‘some woman’ (का अपि स्त्री) — only that her radiance lights up the mountain.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the demons see the Goddess only as a beautiful woman, and the whole embassy that follows rests on that misreading — the divine taken for an object to be acquired.

Verse 5.91

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नैव तादृक् क्वचिद्रूपं दृष्टं केनचिदुत्तमम्।ज्ञायतां काप्यसौ देवी गृह्यतां चासुरेश्वर॥⁠९१⁠॥

naiva tādṛk kvacidrūpaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ kenaciduttamam |jñāyatāṃ kāpyasau devī gṛhyatāṃ cāsureśvara ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

न · एव · तादृक् · क्वचित् · रूपम् · दृष्टम् · केनचित् · उत्तमम्ज्ञायताम् · का · अपि · असौ · देवी · गृह्यताम् · च · असुरेश्वर

na · eva · tādṛk · kvacit · rūpam · dṛṣṭam · kenacit · uttamamjñāyatām · kā · api · asau · devī · gṛhyatām · ca · asureśvara

Word by word

  1. नnanotindeclinable (negation)
  2. एवevaat all, indeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  3. तादृक्tādṛksuchn. nom. sg. of तादृश्; qualifies रूपम्
  4. क्वचित्kvacitanywhere, everindeclinable (indefinite adverb of place)
  5. रूपम्rūpambeauty, formn. nom. sg. of रूप; subject of (अस्ति) with दृष्टम्
  6. दृष्टम्dṛṣṭamhas been seenpast participle (kta) of √दृश् (bhvādi, 1P), n. nom. sg.
  7. केनचित्kenacitby anyoneindefinite pronoun कश्चित्, m. instr. sg.; agent of दृष्टम्
  8. उत्तमम्uttamamsupreme, excellentn. nom. sg. of उत्तम; qualifies रूपम्
  9. ज्ञायताम्jñāyatāmlet it be found out√ज्ञा (kryādi, 9U ‘to know’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
  10. काkāwhointerrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.
  11. अपिapi(with का: ‘who … at all’, ‘whoever’)indeclinable (particle)
  12. असौasauthis, that (yonder)pronoun अदस्, f. nom. sg.; qualifies देवी
  13. देवीdevīgoddessf. nom. sg. of देवी
  14. गृह्यताम्gṛhyatāmlet her be seized√ग्रह् (kryādi, 9U ‘to seize’), karmaṇi prayoga, loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana
  15. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  16. असुरेश्वरasureśvaraO lord of the asurasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)

Translation

Such supreme beauty has never been seen by anyone anywhere. Let it be found out who this goddess is, and let her be seized, O lord of the asuras.

In readable English‘Never has anyone, anywhere, seen beauty so perfect. Find out who this goddess may be, lord of the asuras, and take her.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नैव naiva → न + एव

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + e → ai.

  • क्वचिद्रूपं kvacidrūpaṃ → क्वचित् + रूपम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced r; so also केनचिदुत्तमम् before a vowel.

  • काप्यसौ kāpyasau → का + अपि + असौ

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha, then yaṇ) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    ā + a → ā; i before a becomes y.

  • चासुरेश्वर cāsureśvara → च + असुरेश्वर

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā. Inside the compound, असुर + ईश्वर → असुरेश्वर by guṇa (6.1.87).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरेश्वर asureśvara

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + ईश्वर
    Vigraha
    असुराणाम् ईश्वरः asurāṇām īśvaraḥ
    Literally
    “lord of the asuras”
    In the verse
    Śumbha
Morphology & anvaya

  • तादृक् tādṛk

    Nominative-accusative neuter of तादृश् ‘such’: the final palatal ś appears as k at the end of a word.

  • ज्ञायताम् jñāyatām

    Passive imperative (third person): ‘let it be known’ — a polite command to a king, avoiding the direct second person.

  • गृह्यताम् gṛhyatām

    Passive imperative of √ग्रह्, with samprasāraṇa (ग्रह् → गृह्) before -ya-: ‘let her be taken’.

Anvaya · prose order

तादृक् उत्तमं रूपं केनचित् क्वचित् न एव दृष्टम्। असुरेश्वर, असौ देवी का अपि (इति) ज्ञायतां, (सा) गृह्यतां च।

tādṛk uttamaṃ rūpaṃ kenacit kvacit na eva dṛṣṭam | asureśvara, asau devī kā api (iti) jñāyatāṃ, (sā) gṛhyatāṃ ca |

A negative statement (न एव … दृष्टम्) and two passive imperatives (ज्ञायताम्, गृह्यताम्), with the vocative असुरेश्वर.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The servants press their case: her beauty is beyond anything ever seen, so Śumbha should discover who she is and take her. They call her देवी, ‘goddess’, without understanding the word.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the servants move in one breath from wonder to seizure — ‘find out who she is, and take her’. The story will show how little that grasping understands.

Verse 5.92

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

स्त्रीरत्नमतिचार्वङ्गी द्योतयन्ती दिशस्त्विषा।सा तु तिष्ठति दैत्येन्द्र तां भवान् द्रष्टुमर्हति॥⁠९२⁠॥

strīratnamaticārvaṅgī dyotayantī diśastviṣā |sā tu tiṣṭhati daityendra tāṃ bhavān draṣṭumarhati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

स्त्रीरत्नम् · अतिचार्वङ्गी · द्योतयन्ती · दिशः · त्विषासा · तु · तिष्ठति · दैत्येन्द्र · ताम् · भवान् · द्रष्टुम् · अर्हति

strīratnam · aticārvaṅgī · dyotayantī · diśaḥ · tviṣāsā · tu · tiṣṭhati · daityendra · tām · bhavān · draṣṭum · arhati

Word by word

  1. स्त्रीरत्नम्strīratnama jewel among womenn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate in apposition to सा
  2. अतिचार्वङ्गीaticārvaṅgīexceedingly lovely of limbf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सा
  3. द्योतयन्तीdyotayantīlighting uppresent participle (śatṛ) of the causative of √द्युत् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to shine’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies सा
  4. दिशःdiśaḥthe quarters, all directionsf. acc. pl. of दिश्; object of द्योतयन्ती
  5. त्विषाtviṣāwith her radiancef. instr. sg. of त्विष्
  6. साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of तिष्ठति
  7. तुtuindeed, nowindeclinable (particle)
  8. तिष्ठतिtiṣṭhatiis (there), remains√स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. दैत्येन्द्रdaityendraO lord of the daityasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. ताम्tāmherpronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; object of द्रष्टुम्
  11. भवान्bhavānyour honour, youhonorific pronoun भवत्, m. nom. sg.; subject of अर्हति
  12. द्रष्टुम्draṣṭumto seeinfinitive (tumun) of √दृश्
  13. अर्हतिarhatiought, should√अर्ह् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to deserve, be fit’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

A jewel among women, exceedingly lovely of limb, lighting up the quarters with her radiance — she is there, O lord of the daityas; your honour ought to see her.

In readable English‘A jewel among women, perfect in every limb, she lights the whole horizon with her splendour. There she stays, lord of the demons — you should see her.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चार्वङ्गी cārvaṅgī → चारु + अङ्गी

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    u before a vowel becomes v.

  • दिशस्त्विषा diśastviṣā → दिशः + त्विषा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • तां भवान् tāṃ bhavān → ताम् + भवान्

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • स्त्रीरत्नम् strīratnam

    saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection: ‘the jewel among women’)

    Members
    स्त्री + रत्न
    Vigraha
    स्त्रीषु रत्नम् strīṣu ratnam
    Literally
    “a jewel among women”
    In the verse
    the finest of women — the demons’ way of valuing her as a treasure
    Alternative
    Also analysed as ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, स्त्रीणां रत्नम्; the sense is the same.

  • अतिचार्वङ्गी aticārvaṅgī

    bahuvrīhi (feminine in -ī, as compounds in -अङ्ग take)

    Members
    अति + चारु + अङ्ग
    Vigraha
    अतिचारूणि अङ्गानि यस्याः सा aticārūṇi aṅgāni yasyāḥ sā
    Literally
    “she whose limbs are exceedingly lovely”
    In the verse
    of surpassing grace of form

  • दैत्येन्द्र daityendra

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दैत्य + इन्द्र
    Vigraha
    दैत्यानाम् इन्द्रः daityānām indraḥ
    Literally
    “Indra (chief) of the daityas”
    In the verse
    lord of the demons — Śumbha; a + i → e by guṇa (6.1.87)
Morphology & anvaya

  • द्रष्टुम् draṣṭum

    Infinitive of √दृश् with metathesis of the vowel (दर्श् → द्रष्): द्रष्टुम्. With अर्हति: ‘deserves to see’, i.e. ‘you ought to see’.

  • भवान् bhavān

    The honorific भवत् takes a third-person verb (भवान् … अर्हति), as in 1.60.

  • तिष्ठति tiṣṭhati

    Present of √स्था with the reduplicated stem तिष्ठ-: here ‘is there, stays’, not ‘stands’.

Anvaya · prose order

दैत्येन्द्र, स्त्रीरत्नम् अतिचार्वङ्गी त्विषा दिशः द्योतयन्ती सा तु (तत्र) तिष्ठति; भवान् तां द्रष्टुम् अर्हति।

daityendra, strīratnam aticārvaṅgī tviṣā diśaḥ dyotayantī sā tu (tatra) tiṣṭhati; bhavān tāṃ draṣṭum arhati |

Main clause सा तु तिष्ठति with three descriptions of her in the first line; a second clause भवान् … अर्हति with its infinitive.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The servants describe her a second time and urge Śumbha to go and look. The phrase स्त्रीरत्नम् ‘jewel among women’ prepares the argument that follows: Śumbha owns every jewel of the three worlds (5.93–99), so this one too should be his.

Traditional interpretation

The word ratna governs the whole report and the messenger’s speech that follows (5.109–113): Śumbha is a ‘consumer of jewels’ (रत्नभुजः, 5.112). The Goddess’s light, which the hymn praised, is seen by the demons only as a treasure.

Verse 5.93

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यानि रत्नानि मणयो गजाश्वादीनि वै प्रभो।त्रैलोक्ये तु समस्तानि साम्प्रतं भान्ति ते गृहे॥⁠९३⁠॥

yāni ratnāni maṇayo gajāśvādīni vai prabho |trailokye tu samastāni sāmprataṃ bhānti te gṛhe ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यानि · रत्नानि · मणयः · गजाश्वादीनि · वै · प्रभोत्रैलोक्ये · तु · समस्तानि · साम्प्रतम् · भान्ति · ते · गृहे

yāni · ratnāni · maṇayaḥ · gajāśvādīni · vai · prabhotrailokye · tu · samastāni · sāmpratam · bhānti · te · gṛhe

Word by word

  1. यानिyāniwhatever, whichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. pl.; correlative of the understood तानि
  2. रत्नानिratnānijewels, treasuresn. nom. pl. of रत्न
  3. मणयःmaṇayaḥgemsm. nom. pl. of मणि (i-stem)
  4. गजाश्वादीनिgajāśvādīnielephants, horses and the restn. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  6. प्रभोprabhoO lordm. voc. sg. of प्रभु (u-stem)
  7. त्रैलोक्येtrailokyein the three worldsn. loc. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोक)
  8. तुtuindeedindeclinable (particle; here emphatic, filling the pāda)
  9. समस्तानिsamastāniall of themn. nom. pl. of समस्त; subject of भान्ति
  10. साम्प्रतम्sāmpratamnow, at presentindeclinable (adverb of time)
  11. भान्तिbhāntishine√भा (adādi, 2P ‘to shine’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  12. तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  13. गृहेgṛhein the housen. loc. sg. of गृह

Translation

Whatever jewels, gems, elephants, horses and the like there are in the three worlds, O lord, all of them now shine in your house.

In readable English‘Lord, every treasure of the three worlds — jewels, gems, elephants, horses and all the rest — now shines in your palace.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मणयो गजाश्वादीनि maṇayo gajāśvādīni → मणयः + गजाश्वादीनि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

  • गजाश्वादीनि gajāśvādīni → गज + अश्व + आदीनि

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā and a + ā → ā.

  • साम्प्रतं भान्ति sāmprataṃ bhānti → साम्प्रतम् + भान्ति

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गजाश्वादीनि gajāśvādīni

    bahuvrīhi (its first member गजाश्व is an itaretara dvandva: गजाः च अश्वाः च)

    Members
    गजाश्व + आदि
    Vigraha
    गजाश्वाः आदयः येषां तानि gajāśvāḥ ādayaḥ yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally
    “those of which elephants and horses are the first”
    In the verse
    elephants, horses and other such treasures
Morphology & anvaya

  • यानि yāni

    Neuter plural, though the nouns it gathers are mixed (रत्नानि n., मणयः m.): a relative covering things of different genders is put in the neuter. Its correlative (तानि) is understood with समस्तानि.

  • भान्ति bhānti

    Present of the athematic √भा: भा + अन्ति → भान्ति, ‘they shine’.

  • त्रैलोक्ये trailokye

    Taddhita abstract/collective in -ya with vṛddhi (त्रि → त्रै): त्रैलोक्य ‘the triad of worlds’.

Anvaya · prose order

प्रभो, त्रैलोक्ये यानि रत्नानि मणयः गजाश्वादीनि वै (सन्ति), (तानि) समस्तानि तु साम्प्रतं ते गृहे भान्ति।

prabho, trailokye yāni ratnāni maṇayaḥ gajāśvādīni vai (santi), (tāni) samastāni tu sāmprataṃ te gṛhe bhānti |

A relative clause (यानि … त्रैलोक्ये) gathers the treasures; the main clause (समस्तानि … भान्ति) says where they are now.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The servants begin the catalogue of Śumbha’s plunder (5.93–99). The general statement comes first: every treasure of the three worlds is now in his house.

Traditional interpretation

The list fills out what the chapter’s opening stated briefly: the brothers took over the offices of Indra, the Sun, the Moon, Kubera, Yama, Varuṇa, Vāyu and Agni (5.2–4). Each treasure named below belonged to one of those gods.

Verse 5.94

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ऐरावतः समानीतो गजरत्नं पुरन्दरात्।पारिजाततरुश्चायं तथैवोच्चैःश्रवा हयः॥⁠९४⁠॥

airāvataḥ samānīto gajaratnaṃ purandarāt |pārijātataruścāyaṃ tathaivoccaiḥśravā hayaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ऐरावतः · समानीतः · गजरत्नम् · पुरन्दरात्पारिजाततरुः · च · अयम् · तथा · एव · उच्चैःश्रवाः · हयः

airāvataḥ · samānītaḥ · gajaratnam · purandarātpārijātataruḥ · ca · ayam · tathā · eva · uccaiḥśravāḥ · hayaḥ

Word by word

  1. ऐरावतःairāvataḥAirāvatam. nom. sg.; proper name of Indra’s elephant; subject of समानीतः
  2. समानीतःsamānītaḥhas been broughtpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √नी (bhvādi, 1U ‘to lead’), m. nom. sg.
  3. गजरत्नम्gajaratnamthe jewel of elephantsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to ऐरावतः
  4. पुरन्दरात्purandarātfrom Purandara (Indra)m. abl. sg. of पुरन्दर; compound (see Samāsa)
  5. पारिजाततरुःpārijātataruḥthe Pārijāta treem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  7. अयम्ayamthis (here)pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies पारिजाततरुः
  8. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  9. एवevajust so, tooindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  10. उच्चैःश्रवाःuccaiḥśravāḥUccaiḥśravasm. nom. sg. of उच्चैःश्रवस् (s-stem); compound (see Samāsa); proper name, in apposition to हयः
  11. हयःhayaḥthe horsem. nom. sg. of हय

Translation

Airāvata, the jewel among elephants, has been brought from Purandara; and this Pārijāta tree, and likewise the horse Uccaiḥśravas.

In readable English‘Airāvata, finest of elephants, has been taken from Indra; so too this Pārijāta tree, and the horse Uccaiḥśravas.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • समानीतो गजरत्नं samānīto gajaratnaṃ → समानीतः + गजरत्नम्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced g becomes -o. (ऐरावतः समानीतः keeps its visarga before s.)

  • पारिजाततरुश्चायं pārijātataruścāyaṃ → पारिजाततरुः + च + अयम्

    visarga sandhi, then vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before c becomes s, then ś; च + अयम् → चायम्; the final m becomes anusvāra before t (8.3.23).

  • तथैवोच्चैःश्रवा हयः tathaivoccaiḥśravā hayaḥ → तथा + एव + उच्चैःश्रवाः + हयः

    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi, guṇa), then visarga sandhi · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); आद्गुणः (6.1.87); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    ā + e → ai (तथैव); a + u → o (एवोच्चैः); -āḥ before the voiced h loses its visarga (उच्चैःश्रवा हयः).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गजरत्नम् gajaratnam

    saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)

    Members
    गज + रत्न
    Vigraha
    गजेषु रत्नम् gajeṣu ratnam
    Literally
    “jewel among elephants”
    In the verse
    the finest of elephants
    Alternative
    Or ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, गजानां रत्नम्, with the same sense.

  • पुरन्दरात् purandarāt

    upapada tatpuruṣa (with the augment m: पुरम्-दर)

    Members
    पुर + दर
    Vigraha
    पुरः दारयति इति puraḥ dārayati iti
    Literally
    “the breaker of cities (forts)”
    In the verse
    an epithet of Indra

  • पारिजाततरुः pārijātataruḥ

    karmadhāraya (a name with its class noun)

    Members
    पारिजात + तरु
    Vigraha
    पारिजातः च असौ तरुः च pārijātaḥ ca asau taruḥ ca
    Literally
    “the tree Pārijāta”
    In the verse
    the wish-granting tree of Indra’s heaven

  • उच्चैःश्रवाः uccaiḥśravāḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    उच्चैस् + श्रवस्
    Vigraha
    उच्चैः श्रवसी यस्य सः uccaiḥ śravasī yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose ears are (held) high”
    In the verse
    the name of the divine horse; traditionally also explained as ‘loud-neighing’ (श्रवस् ‘sound, fame’)
Morphology & anvaya

  • समानीतः samānītaḥ

    सम् + आ + √नी + kta: ‘brought together, fetched’. The copula is understood: ‘(has been) brought’.

  • उच्चैःश्रवाः uccaiḥśravāḥ

    Nominative singular of the s-stem उच्चैःश्रवस्: the final -as becomes -āḥ (cf. वेधाः, चन्द्रमाः).

Anvaya · prose order

ऐरावतः गजरत्नं पुरन्दरात् समानीतः; अयं पारिजाततरुः च, तथा एव उच्चैःश्रवाः हयः (समानीतः)।

airāvataḥ gajaratnaṃ purandarāt samānītaḥ; ayaṃ pārijātataruḥ ca, tathā eva uccaiḥśravāḥ hayaḥ (samānītaḥ) |

One participle (समानीतः) serves three subjects; the copula and its repetition are understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first spoils named are Indra’s: his elephant Airāvata, the Pārijāta tree of his heaven, and the horse Uccaiḥśravas.

Traditional interpretation

All three are treasures that came up from the churning of the ocean; the messenger himself says so of the horse (5.110, क्षीरोदमथनोद्भूतम्). Possessing them, Śumbha has taken the visible marks of Indra’s kingship.

Verse 5.95

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

विमानं हंससंयुक्तमेतत्तिष्ठति तेऽङ्गणे।रत्नभूतमिहानीतं यदासीद्वेधसोऽद्भुतम्॥⁠९५⁠॥

vimānaṃ haṃsasaṃyuktametattiṣṭhati te'ṅgaṇe |ratnabhūtamihānītaṃ yadāsīdvedhaso'dbhutam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

विमानम् · हंससंयुक्तम् · एतत् · तिष्ठति · ते · अङ्गणेरत्नभूतम् · इह · आनीतम् · यत् · आसीत् · वेधसः · अद्भुतम्

vimānam · haṃsasaṃyuktam · etat · tiṣṭhati · te · aṅgaṇeratnabhūtam · iha · ānītam · yat · āsīt · vedhasaḥ · adbhutam

Word by word

  1. विमानम्vimānamaerial carn. nom. sg. of विमान; subject of तिष्ठति
  2. हंससंयुक्तम्haṃsasaṃyuktamyoked with swansn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies विमानम्
  3. एतत्etatthispronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies विमानम्
  4. तिष्ठतिtiṣṭhatistands√स्था (bhvādi, 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  5. तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  6. अङ्गणेaṅgaṇein the courtyardn. loc. sg. of अङ्गण
  7. रत्नभूतम्ratnabhūtama very jeweln. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies विमानम्
  8. इहihahereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  9. आनीतम्ānītambroughtpast participle (kta) of आ + √नी (bhvādi, 1U), n. nom. sg.; qualifies विमानम्
  10. यत्yatwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.
  11. आसीत्āsītwas√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  12. वेधसःvedhasaḥof Vedhas (Brahmā, the Creator)m. gen. sg. of वेधस् (s-stem)
  13. अद्भुतम्adbhutamwonder, marveln. nom. sg. of अद्भुत; predicate of आसीत्

Translation

This swan-yoked aerial car, a very jewel, brought here, stands in your courtyard — that which was the wonder of Vedhas.

In readable English‘In your courtyard stands this swan-drawn chariot of the skies, a true treasure brought here — once the marvel of Brahmā.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विमानं हंससंयुक्तमेतत्तिष्ठति vimānaṃ haṃsasaṃyuktametattiṣṭhati → विमानम् + हंससंयुक्तम् + एतत् + तिष्ठति

    anusvāra sandhi; m and t before a vowel and t · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m becomes anusvāra before h; before the vowel of एतत् it stays m; एतत् and तिष्ठति are simply written together.

  • तेऽङ्गणे te'ṅgaṇe → ते + अङ्गणे

    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -e + a- → -e’- (avagraha).

  • इहानीतं ihānītaṃ → इह + आनीतम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • यदासीद्वेधसोऽद्भुतम् yadāsīdvedhaso'dbhutam → यत् + आसीत् + वेधसः + अद्भुतम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing), then visarga sandhi · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel and before v; -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is elided (वेधसोऽद्भुतम्).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हंससंयुक्तम् haṃsasaṃyuktam

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    हंस + संयुक्त
    Vigraha
    हंसैः संयुक्तम् haṃsaiḥ saṃyuktam
    Literally
    “joined with swans”
    In the verse
    drawn by swans — Brahmā’s vehicle

  • रत्नभूतम् ratnabhūtam

    karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, become’ as final member marks identity)

    Members
    रत्न + भूत
    Vigraha
    रत्नं भूतम् ratnaṃ bhūtam
    Literally
    “being a jewel”
    In the verse
    a veritable treasure
    Alternative
    भूत at the end of a compound is sometimes taken as ‘like’ (रत्नम् इव); here identity fits better, since it is being counted among the jewels.
Morphology & anvaya

  • आसीत् āsīt

    Imperfect of √अस् with augment and the inserted ī of this root before -t: आस्-ई-त्, ‘was’.

  • वेधसः vedhasaḥ

    Genitive singular of the s-stem वेधस् ‘the Ordainer’, a name of Brahmā (nom. वेधाः).

Anvaya · prose order

यत् वेधसः अद्भुतम् आसीत्, एतत् हंससंयुक्तं रत्नभूतं विमानम् इह आनीतं ते अङ्गणे तिष्ठति।

yat vedhasaḥ adbhutam āsīt, etat haṃsasaṃyuktaṃ ratnabhūtaṃ vimānam iha ānītaṃ te aṅgaṇe tiṣṭhati |

Main clause विमानम् … तिष्ठति with its qualifiers; a relative clause (यत् … आसीत्) identifies the car’s former owner.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Next is Brahmā’s own vehicle: the aerial car drawn by swans, now parked in Śumbha’s courtyard. Even the Creator has been stripped.

Traditional interpretation

The swan (haṃsa) is Brahmā’s mount in Purāṇic iconography; its car here shows that the demons’ conquest reached beyond Indra’s heaven to the Creator himself.

Verse 5.96

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

निधिरेष महापद्मः समानीतो धनेश्वरात्।किञ्जल्किनीं ददौ चाब्धिर्मालामम्लानपङ्कजाम्॥⁠९६⁠॥

nidhireṣa mahāpadmaḥ samānīto dhaneśvarāt |kiñjalkinīṃ dadau cābdhirmālāmamlānapaṅkajām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

निधिः · एषः · महापद्मः · समानीतः · धनेश्वरात्किञ्जल्किनीम् · ददौ · च · अब्धिः · मालाम् · अम्लानपङ्कजाम्

nidhiḥ · eṣaḥ · mahāpadmaḥ · samānītaḥ · dhaneśvarātkiñjalkinīm · dadau · ca · abdhiḥ · mālām · amlānapaṅkajām

Word by word

  1. निधिःnidhiḥtreasurem. nom. sg. of निधि (i-stem); subject of समानीतः
  2. एषःeṣaḥthispronoun एतद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies निधिः
  3. महापद्मःmahāpadmaḥMahāpadmam. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); name of the treasure, in apposition to निधिः
  4. समानीतःsamānītaḥhas been broughtpast participle (kta) of सम् + आ + √नी, m. nom. sg.
  5. धनेश्वरात्dhaneśvarātfrom the lord of wealth (Kubera)m. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  6. किञ्जल्किनीम्kiñjalkinīmfull of filamentsf. acc. sg. of किञ्जल्किन् (taddhita: ‘having filaments’); qualifies मालाम्
  7. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U ‘to give’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  9. अब्धिःabdhiḥthe Oceanm. nom. sg. of अब्धि (i-stem); subject of ददौ
  10. मालाम्mālāmgarlandf. acc. sg. of माला; object of ददौ
  11. अम्लानपङ्कजाम्amlānapaṅkajāmof unfading lotusesf. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मालाम्

Translation

This treasure, Mahāpadma, has been brought from the lord of wealth; and the Ocean gave a garland full of filaments, of unfading lotuses.

In readable English‘This treasure, the Mahāpadma, was taken from Kubera; and the Ocean gave you a garland of never-fading lotuses, fresh with their filaments.’

‘You’ is supplied: the Ocean’s gift is to Śumbha, as the context makes clear.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • निधिरेष nidhireṣa → निधिः + एषः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After i the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel.

  • एष महापद्मः eṣa mahāpadmaḥ → एषः + महापद्मः

    visarga sandhi (special rule for एषः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)

    The visarga of एषः (as of सः) is dropped before any consonant.

  • समानीतो धनेश्वरात् samānīto dhaneśvarāt → समानीतः + धनेश्वरात्

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced dh becomes -o.

  • चाब्धिर्मालाम् cābdhirmālām → च + अब्धिः + मालाम्

    vowel sandhi, then visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    a + a → ā; then after i the visarga is sounded r before the voiced m.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महापद्मः mahāpadmaḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + पद्म
    Vigraha
    महान् पद्मः mahān padmaḥ
    Literally
    “the great lotus”
    In the verse
    the name of one of Kubera’s treasures (nidhi)

  • धनेश्वरात् dhaneśvarāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    धन + ईश्वर
    Vigraha
    धनस्य ईश्वरः, तस्मात् dhanasya īśvaraḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the lord of wealth”
    In the verse
    from Kubera; a + ī → e by guṇa (6.1.87)

  • अम्लानपङ्कजाम् amlānapaṅkajām

    bahuvrīhi (अम्लान is a nañ tatpuruṣa, न म्लानम्; पङ्कज an upapada tatpuruṣa, पङ्के जायते इति)

    Members
    अम्लान + पङ्कज
    Vigraha
    अम्लानानि पङ्कजानि यस्यां सा, ताम् amlānāni paṅkajāni yasyāṃ sā, tām
    Literally
    “(a garland) in which the mud-born (lotuses) are unwithered”
    In the verse
    a garland of lotuses that never fade
Morphology & anvaya

  • ददौ dadau

    Perfect of √दा: reduplicated द-दौ, with the -au ending that ā-roots take in the third singular.

  • किञ्जल्किनीम् kiñjalkinīm

    Possessive taddhita in -in (किञ्जल्क ‘filament of a lotus’ + इनि), feminine -inī: ‘having filaments’, i.e. of fresh, full-blown flowers.

Anvaya · prose order

एषः महापद्मः निधिः धनेश्वरात् समानीतः; अब्धिः च किञ्जल्किनीम् अम्लानपङ्कजां मालां ददौ।

eṣaḥ mahāpadmaḥ nidhiḥ dhaneśvarāt samānītaḥ; abdhiḥ ca kiñjalkinīm amlānapaṅkajāṃ mālāṃ dadau |

Two clauses: a participial one (निधिः … समानीतः) and a finite one with ददौ, whose object मालाम् has two qualifiers.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Kubera, lord of wealth, has lost the Mahāpadma, and the Ocean has given a garland of unfading lotuses. The Ocean’s gift is phrased as a gift (ददौ), not a seizure: the conquered gods now pay tribute.

Traditional interpretation

Classical lists of Kubera’s nine treasures (nidhi) include Mahāpadma. Its loss marks the seizure of Kubera’s office, one of those named at the chapter’s opening (कौबेरम्, 5.3).

Verse 5.97

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

छत्रं ते वारुणं गेहे काञ्चनस्रावि तिष्ठति।तथायं स्यन्दनवरो यः पुराऽऽसीत्प्रजापतेः॥⁠९७⁠॥

chatraṃ te vāruṇaṃ gehe kāñcanasrāvi tiṣṭhati |tathāyaṃ syandanavaro yaḥ purā''sītprajāpateḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

छत्रम् · ते · वारुणम् · गेहे · काञ्चनस्रावि · तिष्ठतितथा · अयम् · स्यन्दनवरः · यः · पुरा · आसीत् · प्रजापतेः

chatram · te · vāruṇam · gehe · kāñcanasrāvi · tiṣṭhatitathā · ayam · syandanavaraḥ · yaḥ · purā · āsīt · prajāpateḥ

Word by word

  1. छत्रम्chatramparasol, royal umbrellan. nom. sg. of छत्र; subject of तिष्ठति
  2. तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
  3. वारुणम्vāruṇamVaruṇa’sn. nom. sg. of वारुण (taddhita: ‘belonging to Varuṇa’); qualifies छत्रम्
  4. गेहेgehein the housen. loc. sg. of गेह
  5. काञ्चनस्राविkāñcanasrāvistreaming goldn. nom. sg. of काञ्चनस्राविन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies छत्रम्
  6. तिष्ठतिtiṣṭhatistands√स्था (bhvādi, 1P), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  8. अयम्ayamthis (here)pronoun इदम्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies स्यन्दनवरः
  9. स्यन्दनवरःsyandanavaraḥfinest of chariotsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. यःyaḥwhichrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.
  11. पुराpurāformerlyindeclinable (adverb of time)
  12. आसीत्āsītwas√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  13. प्रजापतेःprajāpateḥPrajāpati’sm. gen. sg. of प्रजापति (i-stem); compound (see Samāsa); predicate (possessor) with आसीत्

Translation

Varuṇa’s parasol, streaming gold, stands in your house; likewise this finest of chariots, which formerly was Prajāpati’s.

In readable English‘Varuṇa’s parasol, raining gold, stands in your palace, and so does this superb chariot that once belonged to Prajāpati.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • छत्रं ते वारुणं गेहे chatraṃ te vāruṇaṃ gehe → छत्रम् + ते + वारुणम् + गेहे

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra, twice.

  • तथायं tathāyaṃ → तथा + अयम्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

  • स्यन्दनवरो यः syandanavaro yaḥ → स्यन्दनवरः + यः

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.

  • पुराऽऽसीत् purā''sīt → पुरा + आसीत्

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that a long ā has been absorbed; it is not pronounced.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • काञ्चनस्रावि kāñcanasrāvi

    upapada tatpuruṣa (with the agent affix -in, ṇini)

    Members
    काञ्चन + स्राविन्
    Vigraha
    काञ्चनं स्रवति इति kāñcanaṃ sravati iti
    Literally
    “that which streams gold”
    In the verse
    showering gold — Varuṇa’s parasol

  • स्यन्दनवरः syandanavaraḥ

    saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)

    Members
    स्यन्दन + वर
    Vigraha
    स्यन्दनेषु वरः syandaneṣu varaḥ
    Literally
    “the best among chariots”
    In the verse
    a superb chariot

  • प्रजापतेः prajāpateḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    प्रजा + पति
    Vigraha
    प्रजानां पतिः, तस्य prajānāṃ patiḥ, tasya
    Literally
    “of the lord of creatures”
    In the verse
    Prajāpati, the creator-lord
Morphology & anvaya

  • काञ्चनस्रावि kāñcanasrāvi

    Neuter nominative of the -in stem स्राविन् (√स्रु + ṇini, with vṛddhi): the final n is dropped, leaving -स्रावि.

  • वारुणम् vāruṇam

    Taddhita in -a with vṛddhi of the first vowel (वरुण → वारुण): ‘of Varuṇa’, cf. कौबेरम्, याम्यम् in 5.3.

Anvaya · prose order

ते गेहे वारुणं काञ्चनस्रावि छत्रं तिष्ठति; तथा यः पुरा प्रजापतेः आसीत्, अयं स्यन्दनवरः (ते गेहे तिष्ठति)।

te gehe vāruṇaṃ kāñcanasrāvi chatraṃ tiṣṭhati; tathā yaḥ purā prajāpateḥ āsīt, ayaṃ syandanavaraḥ (te gehe tiṣṭhati) |

Two parallel statements of place; in the second the verb is understood, and a relative clause names the chariot’s former owner.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Varuṇa’s gold-showering parasol and Prajāpati’s chariot complete the list of royal insignia. The parasol is the classic sign of sovereignty; its owner has lost his rule (वरुणस्य, 5.3).

Verse 5.98

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मृत्योरुत्क्रान्तिदा नाम शक्तिरीश त्वया हृता।पाशः सलिलराजस्य भ्रातुस्तव परिग्रहे॥⁠९८⁠॥

mṛtyorutkrāntidā nāma śaktirīśa tvayā hṛtā |pāśaḥ salilarājasya bhrātustava parigrahe ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मृत्योः · उत्क्रान्तिदा · नाम · शक्तिः · ईश · त्वया · हृतापाशः · सलिलराजस्य · भ्रातुः · तव · परिग्रहे

mṛtyoḥ · utkrāntidā · nāma · śaktiḥ · īśa · tvayā · hṛtāpāśaḥ · salilarājasya · bhrātuḥ · tava · parigrahe

Word by word

  1. मृत्योःmṛtyoḥof Death (Yama)m. gen. sg. of मृत्यु (u-stem); possessor of शक्तिः
  2. उत्क्रान्तिदाutkrāntidāUtkrāntidā, ‘she who gives departure (of life)’f. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); the weapon’s name
  3. नामnāmanamed, by nameindeclinable
  4. शक्तिःśaktiḥspear (śakti weapon)f. nom. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); subject of हृता
  5. ईशīśaO lordm. voc. sg. of ईश
  6. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of हृता
  7. हृताhṛtāhas been takenpast participle (kta) of √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take, carry off’), f. nom. sg.
  8. पाशःpāśaḥthe noosem. nom. sg. of पाश; subject (with अस्ति understood)
  9. सलिलराजस्यsalilarājasyaof the king of the waters (Varuṇa)m. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  10. भ्रातुःbhrātuḥof the brotherm. gen. sg. of भ्रातृ (ṛ-stem); depends on परिग्रहे
  11. तवtavayoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, gen. sg.; qualifies भ्रातुः
  12. परिग्रहेparigrahein the possessionm. loc. sg. of परिग्रह

Translation

The spear of Death named Utkrāntidā has been taken by you, O lord; the noose of the king of the waters is in your brother’s possession.

In readable English‘You have taken, lord, Death’s spear called Utkrāntidā; and the noose of Varuṇa, king of the waters, is held by your brother.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मृत्योरुत्क्रान्तिदा mṛtyorutkrāntidā → मृत्योः + उत्क्रान्तिदा

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After o (not a/ā) the visarga (as रु) is sounded r before a vowel.

  • शक्तिरीश śaktirīśa → शक्तिः + ईश

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After i the visarga is sounded r before a vowel.

  • भ्रातुस्तव bhrātustava → भ्रातुः + तव

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • उत्क्रान्तिदा utkrāntidā

    upapada tatpuruṣa (√दा with the affix -a, feminine -ā)

    Members
    उत्क्रान्ति + दा
    Vigraha
    उत्क्रान्तिं ददाति इति utkrāntiṃ dadāti iti
    Literally
    “giver of the going-out”
    In the verse
    the spear that makes the life-breath depart — Yama’s weapon

  • सलिलराजस्य salilarājasya

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (राजन् becomes the a-stem राज at the end of a compound)

    Members
    सलिल + राजन्
    Vigraha
    सलिलानां राजा, तस्य salilānāṃ rājā, tasya
    Literally
    “of the king of the waters”
    In the verse
    of Varuṇa
Morphology & anvaya

  • भ्रातुः bhrātuḥ

    Genitive singular of the ṛ-stem भ्रातृ: -ṛ + -as → -uḥ (cf. पितुः, मातुः).

  • हृता hṛtā

    Feminine past participle of √हृ agreeing with शक्तिः, used with the agent instrumental त्वया as a passive past: ‘was taken by you’.

Anvaya · prose order

ईश, मृत्योः उत्क्रान्तिदा नाम शक्तिः त्वया हृता; सलिलराजस्य पाशः तव भ्रातुः परिग्रहे (अस्ति)।

īśa, mṛtyoḥ utkrāntidā nāma śaktiḥ tvayā hṛtā; salilarājasya pāśaḥ tava bhrātuḥ parigrahe (asti) |

Two clauses: a passive past (शक्तिः … त्वया हृता) and a nominal sentence of place with the copula understood (पाशः … परिग्रहे).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Now the weapons: Yama’s spear Utkrāntidā, ‘giver of the soul’s departure’, taken by Śumbha, and Varuṇa’s noose, held by Niśumbha. Śumbha and his brother between them hold the instruments of death and of binding.

Traditional interpretation

Both seizures fill out the opening statement of the chapter, that the brothers took the offices of Yama (याम्यम्) and Varuṇa (वरुणस्य) (5.3).

Verse 5.99

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

निशुम्भस्याब्धिजाताश्च समस्ता रत्नजातयः।वह्निरपि ददौ तुभ्यमग्निशौचे च वाससी॥⁠९९⁠॥

niśumbhasyābdhijātāśca samastā ratnajātayaḥ |vahnirapi dadau tubhyamagniśauce ca vāsasī ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

निशुम्भस्य · अब्धिजाताः · च · समस्ताः · रत्नजातयःवह्निः · अपि · ददौ · तुभ्यम् · अग्निशौचे · च · वाससी

niśumbhasya · abdhijātāḥ · ca · samastāḥ · ratnajātayaḥvahniḥ · api · dadau · tubhyam · agniśauce · ca · vāsasī

Word by word

  1. निशुम्भस्यniśumbhasyaNiśumbha’s, (in the possession) of Niśumbham. gen. sg. of निशुम्भ; predicate of possession
  2. अब्धिजाताःabdhijātāḥborn of the oceanf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रत्नजातयः
  3. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  4. समस्ताःsamastāḥallf. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies रत्नजातयः
  5. रत्नजातयःratnajātayaḥkinds of jewelsf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  6. वह्निःvahniḥFire (Agni)m. nom. sg. of वह्नि (i-stem); subject of ददौ
  7. अपिapitoo, evenindeclinable (particle)
  8. ददौdadaugave√दा (juhotyādi, 3U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. तुभ्यम्tubhyamto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg.
  10. अग्निशौचेagniśaucepurified by firen. acc. du.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वाससी
  11. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  12. वाससीvāsasītwo garmentsn. acc. du. of वासस् (s-stem); object of ददौ

Translation

And all the kinds of jewels born of the ocean are Niśumbha’s; Fire too gave you a pair of garments purified by fire.

In readable English‘Every kind of gem the ocean yields belongs to Niśumbha; and even Agni has given you two garments purified by fire.’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • निशुम्भस्याब्धिजाताश्च niśumbhasyābdhijātāśca → निशुम्भस्य + अब्धिजाताः + च

    vowel sandhi, then visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    a + a → ā; visarga before c becomes s, then ś.

  • समस्ता रत्नजातयः samastā ratnajātayaḥ → समस्ताः + रत्नजातयः

    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant loses its visarga (रु → y, dropped before the consonant).

  • वह्निरपि vahnirapi → वह्निः + अपि

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After i the visarga is sounded r before a vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • अब्धिजाताः abdhijātāḥ

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    अब्धि + जात
    Vigraha
    अब्धेः जाताः abdheḥ jātāḥ
    Literally
    “born from the ocean”
    In the verse
    ocean-born gems (pearls, coral and the like)
    Alternative
    Also saptamī tatpuruṣa, अब्धौ जाताः ‘born in the ocean’; the sense is the same.

  • रत्नजातयः ratnajātayaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रत्न + जाति
    Vigraha
    रत्नानां जातयः ratnānāṃ jātayaḥ
    Literally
    “the kinds of jewels”
    In the verse
    every sort of precious stone

  • अग्निशौचे agniśauce

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    अग्नि + शौच
    Vigraha
    अग्निना शौचं ययोः ते agninā śaucaṃ yayoḥ te
    Literally
    “(two garments) whose cleansing is by fire”
    In the verse
    garments purified by fire, a mark of divine cloth
Morphology & anvaya

  • वाससी vāsasī

    Neuter dual of the s-stem वासस् ‘garment’: -as + -ī → -असी, ‘a pair of garments’ (upper and lower cloth).

  • तुभ्यम् tubhyam

    Dative singular of युष्मद् — the full form, beside the enclitic ते used in 5.93–97.

Anvaya · prose order

अब्धिजाताः समस्ताः रत्नजातयः च निशुम्भस्य (सन्ति); वह्निः अपि तुभ्यम् अग्निशौचे वाससी च ददौ।

abdhijātāḥ samastāḥ ratnajātayaḥ ca niśumbhasya (santi); vahniḥ api tubhyam agniśauce vāsasī ca dadau |

A nominal sentence of possession (genitive निशुम्भस्य with the copula understood), then a finite clause with ददौ and its dative and object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The catalogue ends with the ocean’s gems, held by Niśumbha, and Agni’s gift of fire-cleansed garments to Śumbha. Like the Ocean in 5.96, Agni gives: the conquered gods now serve. In the digital text a footnote asterisk of the printed book followed वह्निरपि; it has been removed (corrections.json, encoding).

Traditional interpretation

Agni’s office (वह्निकर्म, 5.4) was among those the brothers seized. Garments ‘purified by fire’ are traditionally understood as cloth that fire cleanses without burning.

Verse 5.100

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एवं दैत्येन्द्र रत्नानि समस्तान्याहृतानि ते।स्त्रीरत्नमेषा कल्याणी त्वया कस्मान्न गृह्यते॥⁠१००⁠॥

evaṃ daityendra ratnāni samastānyāhṛtāni te |strīratnameṣā kalyāṇī tvayā kasmānna gṛhyate ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एवम् · दैत्येन्द्र · रत्नानि · समस्तानि · आहृतानि · तेस्त्रीरत्नम् · एषा · कल्याणी · त्वया · कस्मात् · न · गृह्यते

evam · daityendra · ratnāni · samastāni · āhṛtāni · testrīratnam · eṣā · kalyāṇī · tvayā · kasmāt · na · gṛhyate

Word by word

  1. एवम्evamthusindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  2. दैत्येन्द्रdaityendraO lord of the daityasm. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.92)
  3. रत्नानिratnānijewels, treasuresn. nom. pl. of रत्न; subject
  4. समस्तानिsamastānialln. nom. pl. of समस्त; qualifies रत्नानि
  5. आहृतानिāhṛtānihave been broughtpast participle (kta) of आ + √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to fetch’), n. nom. pl.
  6. तेtefor you, to yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic dat./gen. sg.
  7. स्त्रीरत्नम्strīratnama jewel among womenn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa, 5.92); in apposition to एषा
  8. एषाeṣāthis (woman)pronoun एतद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of गृह्यते
  9. कल्याणीkalyāṇīlovely, auspiciousf. nom. sg. of कल्याण (feminine in -ī); qualifies एषा

  12. नnanotindeclinable (negation)
  13. गृह्यतेgṛhyateis taken√ग्रह् (kryādi, 9U), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

Translation

Thus, O lord of the daityas, all the jewels have been brought to you; why is this lovely one, a jewel among women, not taken by you?

In readable English‘So, lord of the demons, every treasure has been brought to you. Why then do you not take this lovely one, the jewel among women?’

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एवं दैत्येन्द्र evaṃ daityendra → एवम् + दैत्येन्द्र

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • समस्तान्याहृतानि samastānyāhṛtāni → समस्तानि + आहृतानि

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i before a dissimilar vowel becomes y.

  • कस्मान्न kasmānna → कस्मात् + न

    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    Final t before a nasal becomes the nasal n (optional, but usual).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्येन्द्र daityendra

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दैत्य + इन्द्र
    Vigraha
    दैत्यानाम् इन्द्रः daityānām indraḥ
    Literally
    “chief of the daityas”
    In the verse

  • स्त्रीरत्नम् strīratnam

    saptamī tatpuruṣa (of selection)

    Members
    स्त्री + रत्न
    Vigraha
    स्त्रीषु रत्नम् strīṣu ratnam
    Literally

    In the verse
    as in 5.92
Morphology & anvaya

  • कस्मात् kasmāt

    Ablative of किम् used as ‘why’ (from what cause), with the negative: ‘why … not?’.

  • गृह्यते gṛhyate

    Passive present of √ग्रह् with samprasāraṇa (ग्रह् → गृह्-य-ते), as in गृह्यताम् (5.91).

Anvaya · prose order

दैत्येन्द्र, एवं समस्तानि रत्नानि ते आहृतानि; एषा कल्याणी स्त्रीरत्नं त्वया कस्मात् न गृह्यते?

daityendra, evaṃ samastāni ratnāni te āhṛtāni; eṣā kalyāṇī strīratnaṃ tvayā kasmāt na gṛhyate?

A summary statement (रत्नानि … आहृतानि) and a rhetorical question in the passive (एषा … कस्मात् न गृह्यते).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The servants close their report with its conclusion: every ratna is already Śumbha’s, so the one ratna still missing, the Goddess, should be taken too. The speech that began in 5.90 ends here.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: the argument from possession is the demons’ undoing. Having taken everything, they assume the Goddess can be taken like the rest.
